# SiteWise Authentication Audit — Verdict

**Branch** `joel` · **HEAD** `698a1cc` · **Deployment** unified FastAPI on `127.0.0.1:8090` (I restarted it; it had stopped).

**Overall: BLOCKED — partial.** Token verification and RBAC/MFA enforcement are genuinely solid and evidenced. **Identity reconciliation between Supabase and local PostgreSQL is broken**, and that is the root cause of the symptom you described.

---

## A. Root causes (evidence-backed)

### P0 — Unseen identities are provisioned `active`, ignoring authoritative status

[user_repository.py:97](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/backend/src/sitewise/repositories/user_repository.py#L97) hardcodes `role=rbac.ROLE_ENTREPRENEUR, account_status=rbac.STATUS_ACTIVE` for **any** UUID not already mirrored.

Live proof — this authoritative account is `pending` and has no local row:

```
02183dc8-9f4e-4273-a8bd-afbcd705eb4b  supabase: entrepreneur/pending  joelcode@gmail.com
→ first sign-in mints local entrepreneur/ACTIVE
```

Impact: application-level pending/suspended status is bypassed on first login. (A Supabase *auth ban* still blocks token issuance, so this is not a full authentication bypass — it is an authorization-status bypass.)

### P1 — Role and status never reconcile on login

[user_service.py:73](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/backend/src/sitewise/services/user_service.py#L73) syncs **only** `email_verified`. Since the backend reads role/status from the local row, these two real administrators are locked out of `/admin`:

| UUID | Local | Supabase | Email |
|---|---|---|---|
| `3911bb2e-c829-4578-831a-5b01fd1ddefa` | `entrepreneur` | **`admin`** | admin.sitewise@gmail.com |
| `9cc08037-4930-491d-b22a-63e03735f99a` | `entrepreneur` | **`admin`** | admin.sitewise1@gmail.com |

The working admin is `7947affa` (sitewise.fyp@gmail.com) — created by a provisioning script, not by login. Both broken rows have `last_login == created_at`, confirming they were minted by the login path.

### P1 — `email_verified` can never sync

[security.py:176](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/backend/src/sitewise/core/security.py#L176) reads `claims.get("email_verified")`. **Supabase access tokens do not carry that claim** (confirmed against Supabase's JWT docs: `iss, exp, sub, role, email, phone, aal, amr, session_id, app_metadata, user_metadata`). So it is always `None` on the JWKS path and the sync branch never runs. 6 real accounts are stuck at `False`.

The test that "proves" this works — `test_unverified_email_syncs_from_token` — passes only because [conftest.py:95](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/backend/tests/conftest.py#L95) **injects `email_verified: True` into the fixture token**. A test-fidelity gap, not a working feature.

### P1 — Mobile transport can send an expired token

[mobile_api_client.dart:32](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/fyp_mobile_app/lib/data/repositories/mobile_api_client.dart#L32) uses the synchronous `currentSession?.accessToken`, and `_send` has **no 401 → refresh → retry** ([line 117](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/fyp_mobile_app/lib/data/repositories/mobile_api_client.dart#L117) maps 401 straight to `UnauthorizedFailure`). The auth repository's own comment at line 170 documents this exact trap and correctly uses `getSession()` — the transport does not.

### P2 — `sitewise_token` cookie (your specific audit target)

[auth_client.js:335](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/fyp_jaspr_web/tool/auth_client.js#L335):

| Property | Value | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| `HttpOnly` | **absent** (JS-written) | readable by any XSS |
| `Secure` | only when `https:` | correct for localhost dev |
| `SameSite` | `Lax` | adequate |
| `Path` | `/` | rides **every** same-origin request, incl. static assets |
| `Max-Age` | `28800` (8 h fixed) | **does not track JWT `exp`** (Supabase default 1 h) |

**Why it exists:** the SSR pass cannot read `localStorage`, so `installServerSession` reads the token from this cookie. **But SSR is dormant in your default deployment** — `run-sitewise.ps1` serves the compiled client bundle through FastAPI; `createStaffGuard` only runs under the separate `serve.cmd` → `jaspr serve` launcher. So in normal operation the cookie provides **no function** while still exposing the bearer token.

Mitigating fact: [staff_guard.dart:89](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/fyp_jaspr_web/lib/data/staff_guard.dart#L89) re-validates the token against `/api/v1/auth/me` and **rewrites** the unsigned session cookie from the verified response — a forged cookie is never an authorization input. That part is correctly designed.

### P2 — No server-side route guard in the default deployment

Runtime, anonymous, no cookies:

```
/admin          200 text/html      /agent   200 text/html      /entrepreneur  200 text/html
```

The SPA shell renders; the client router then redirects. **All data remains protected** (11/11 API probes → 401). Acceptable defence-in-depth posture, but "unauthorized-route protection" is client-side only in this mode.

### P2 — Two stale JS tests (not a product bug)

`test_callback_flows.js` **16/18**. Both failures assert the *pre-`joel10`* invitation contract (local sign-out + "awaiting administrator activation" notice). [auth_client.js:1151](file:///C:/Users/Lenovo/AndroidStudioProjects/FYP/fyp_jaspr_web/tool/auth_client.js#L1151) intentionally replaced that with `finishStaffOnboarding()` → TOTP enrollment → backend activation. Git confirms: test last touched `ddadce9 joel9`, implementation changed in `c1e69d8 joel10` / `8f7b26e joel15`.

### P2 — Hygiene

`last_login` write-once at creation · `secret_key="change-me"` dead config · hardcoded default DB credentials in `config.py` · `_ALLOWED_ALGORITHMS` constant unused (verifier defaults to ES256 only) · duplicate import `dependencies.py:269` · `staff.py` docstring names the stale `sitesense` DB · `run-sitewise.ps1:45` points at `fyp_mobile_app/config/dev.env` **which does not exist** · `assets/config/dev.json` carries `SITEWISE_DEV_AUTH_BYPASS: "true"` but is read only by dead `config_loader.dart` (live path reads `sitewise_dev.json` = `false`) · **no unit tests** for the mobile auth repository, `AuthNotifier`, or web refresh logic.

## B. What genuinely PASSES

| Area | Evidence |
|---|---|
| JWT verification | **15/15** `test_security.py`: expired, wrong iss/aud, unknown `kid`, HS256 forgery, unsigned, tampered, missing `sub`/`aud`, future `nbf`, malformed, JWKS unreachable→503, empty JWKS fail-closed, **key rotation** |
| Algorithm allow-list | Runtime: `alg:none` → `401 "disallowed signing algorithm"` — rejected **before** any key lookup |
| RBAC + MFA | **108/108** backend tests (`test_rbac_domain`, `test_staff_authorization`, `test_staff_mfa_rbac`, `test_auth_api`, `test_staff_admin_api`) |
| Staff assurance logic | **32/32** `test_staff_assurance.js` (enroll/verify routing, portal separation, unknown-role fail-closed) |
| SSR guard | **23/23** Jaspr tests (`staff_guard`, `login_page`, `staff_security_shell`) |
| Anonymous API denial | 11/11 protected endpoints → `401 {"detail":"Sign in to continue."}` — no leakage |
| Config consistency | web `auth-config.json` == backend URL **and** publishable key; **no secret-looking values in public assets**; secret key server-side only |
| JWKS | reachable, 1 key: `kid=b4b6694b…`, `EC/ES256/P-256/sig` |
| Dev bypass | **compiled out** of the served bundle (0 matches for `dev-bypass`/`Local Developer`) |
| CSRF | backend reads **zero** cookies — Bearer-only, so not applicable |
| Cross-tab sync | auth-js 2.118.0 `BroadcastChannel` broadcasts all events; app clears cookies + navigates on `SIGNED_OUT` |
| Refresh concurrency | gotrue 2.27.3 `_callRefreshToken` de-duplicates in-flight refreshes; `_doRefresh` discards stale results via `_sessionVersion`; distinguishes retryable vs rejected refresh |
| Mobile storage | Keystore/Keychain via `SecureSessionLocalStorage`, PKCE, `autoRefreshToken: true`, `detectSessionInUri: false`, **no MFA** (as required) |
| Mobile logout | gotrue removes the persisted session **before** the network call; `AuthNotifier.signOut` locks the UI even on failure |
| Google cancellation | `_initializeGoogleSignIn` clears the cached slot on failure; cancellation never touches it → button retryable |

---

## C. Token & session architecture

```
Supabase GoTrue ──issues──> access JWT (ES256, ~1h) + refresh token
   │
   ├─ WEB: supabase-js 2.118.0, persistSession+autoRefresh, PKCE
   │        storage = localStorage (remember) | sessionStorage (no remember)
   │        apiRequest() → getSession() → Authorization: Bearer …
   │        mirror → sitewise_token + sitewise_session cookies (SSR only)
   │
   └─ MOBILE: gotrue 2.27.3, Keystore/Keychain, PKCE, autoRefresh
            Google: native ID token → signInWithIdToken (no deep link)
            transport reads currentSession (⚠ P1)
   │
FastAPI: bearer_token → JWKS verify (sig/alg/iss/aud/exp/nbf/sub)
         → get_current_user (local mirror by UUID)
         → get_active_user (403 suspended/pending)
         → get_staff_user (staff role + aal2, else 403 staff_mfa_required)
   │
Role/status: ALWAYS from the local DB row, never token metadata  ← correct
```

Logout: SDK clears persisted session → `SIGNED_OUT` → cookies cleared → navigate to `/login`. Mobile: Keystore entry deleted, UI locked regardless of network outcome.

| C2 local-only UUIDs | **103** — test fixtures |

| C4 status mismatches | 0 |
| C5 `email_verified` mismatches | **6** (local `False`, Supabase `True`) |
| C6 email mismatches | 0 |
| C8 local distribution | admin/active 5 · agent/active 3 · entrepreneur/active 102 |
| C9 staff rows with no Supabase identity | **5** (4 `admin`, 1 `agent`) — all fixtures |

Your two agents are correctly mirrored: `8de9ed3b…` and `dcbc87df…` both local `agent`, matching Supabase, `last_login=NULL` (provisioned, not login-minted).

The 103 fixtures (`geo-*`, `legacy-retired-*`, `verify-*`, `ml-sel-*`, `pu47-*`, `mfa-repro@sitewise-test.invalid`) **cannot authenticate** — no Supabase identity means no token. They are inert but pollute admin counts. **Not deleted**, per your constraint.

Read-only SQL to reproduce:

```sql
SELECT user_id, email, role, account_status, email_verified, created_at, last_login
FROM public.users
WHERE email NOT LIKE '%jwks-test.example'
  AND email NOT LIKE '%sitewise-test.invalid'
ORDER BY created_at;
```

## G. Proposed fixes — **awaiting your approval, nothing applied**

**P0**
1. `get_or_create_from_supabase`: accept authoritative `role` + `account_status` and stop hardcoding `entrepreneur/active`. Fail closed (reject provisioning) when the authoritative row is absent rather than inventing a status.
   *Test:* unseen UUID whose Supabase row is `pending` → local row `pending`, `/auth/session` → 403.

**P1**
2. `get_or_sync_current`: reconcile `role` and `account_status` from the authoritative Supabase row on every login, with an audit event on change. Repair the 2 admin rows **only after you confirm those UUIDs are genuinely yours**.
   *Test:* Supabase `admin` / local `entrepreneur` → after login local `admin`, discrepancy reported.
3. Source `email_verified` from the Supabase admin API's `email_confirmed_at` (as `staff.py` already does), not from a claim that does not exist. Fix `conftest.py` to stop injecting it.
4. `MobileApiClient._bearer()` → `await getSession()`; add a single 401 → refresh → retry.
   *Test:* expired token → one refresh → request succeeds; rejected refresh → clean sign-out.

**P2**
5. `sitewise_token`: either stop writing it in client-mode builds, or move to a backend-set `HttpOnly` cookie with `Max-Age` derived from `exp`. **Requires assessing SSR impact first** — I have not touched it.
6. Align the cookie's lifetime with the JWT, or drop it when SSR is inactive.
7. Update the 2 stale `test_callback_flows.js` assertions to the `finishStaffOnboarding` contract.
8. Update `last_login` on each successful login.
9. Remove dead config/code; fix `run-sitewise.ps1:45` and the stale `sitesense` docstring.
10. Add mobile auth-repository and web refresh unit tests.
11. Review the 103 fixture rows' dependencies, then quarantine them — **not before**.

**Bottom line:** the cryptographic and authorization layers are sound and provably fail closed. The chain breaks at **Local identity reconciliation** — Supabase says `admin`, the enforcement mirror says `entrepreneur`, and nothing ever corrects it. That single gap explains your reported symptom, and the same code path will silently activate a pending account on first login.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Deployment:** ONE unified FastAPI server on **:8090** (`run-sitewise.cmd` → `run-sitewise.ps1` → `uvicorn sitewise.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8090`, cwd `backend`, `PYTHONPATH=backend/src`, `PYTHONUTF8=1`, `NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1,::1`). Serves the compiled client-mode Jaspr bundle from `fyp_jaspr_web/build/jaspr` AND `/api/v1/*`, `/health`, `/docs`, `/openapi.json` on the same origin. **Does NOT rebuild Jaspr or Flutter.**
   - **The SSR flow is a SEPARATE, dormant, opt-in launcher:** `fyp_jaspr_web/serve.cmd` → `serve.impl.ps1` → `jaspr serve`, which runs `main.server.dart` with `createApiProxy(kBackendApiBase)` + `createStaffGuard(kBackendApiBase)` shelf middleware. In the default `run-sitewise` deployment there is **no server-side route guard** — `frontend.py::mount_frontend` does plain SPA fallback.
   - Browser API base is same-origin: `<meta name="sitewise-api" content="">`; `lib/data/api_config.dart` holds server-only `kBackendApiBase` (default `http://127.0.0.1:8000`, override `SITEWISE_API_BASE`) and `kPublicApiBase`.
   - `tool/gen_auth_config.js` generates `web/auth-config.json` from `backend/.env`, emitting ONLY `supabaseUrl` + `supabasePublishableKey`; refuses `sb_secret_`/`service_role`.
   - **Backend auth chain:** `bearer_token` → `get_jwt_verifier()` → `SupabaseJWTVerifier.verify` (local JWKS) or `SupabaseUserinfoVerifier` fallback → `get_current_user` (email claim, UUID subject, `UserService.get_or_sync_current`) → `get_active_user` (403 suspended/non-active) → `get_staff_user` (`rbac.is_staff` + `aal == "aal2"`, else structured 403 `{"code":"staff_mfa_required"}`) → `require_admin_mfa` / `require_agent_mfa`. Aliases `CurrentUser`, `ActiveUser`, `StaffUser`, `AgentMfaUser`, `AdminMfaUser`.
   - **Role/status ALWAYS read from the local DB row, never token metadata.**
   - JWT: `_ALLOWED_ALGORITHMS = {"ES256","RS256","PS256"}` (module constant, unused); verifier default `{"ES256"}`; `_REQUIRED_CLAIMS = ("sub","exp","iat","aud","iss")`; leeway 30; alg checked BEFORE key lookup. `JWKSClient` caches 600s, force-refreshes once on unknown `kid`, fails closed with `AuthUnavailableError`.
   - Live JWKS: 1 key `kid=b4b6694b-02e6-4704-81bd-c8b299bcd398`, `kty=EC alg=ES256 crv=P-256 use=sig`.
   - **Two databases:** local PostgreSQL `Final_Location_Analysis` (enforcement mirror, 110 `public.users` rows) and remote Supabase project `lzdpjwmcjbhtkzkqudyt` (authoritative `public.users`, 11 rows; plus `public.agent`, `public.company`, `public.role_change_audit`, `public.staff_invitations`). `public.role_change_audit` does NOT exist locally.
   - Three-role model: `rbac.ALL_ROLES = (entrepreneur, agent, admin)`; `LEGACY_ROLE_ALIASES = {user→entrepreneur, property_owner→entrepreneur, administrator→admin}`; `ACCOUNT_STATUSES = (pending, active, suspended)`; `REQUIRED_STAFF_AAL = "aal2"`.
   - **Resolved SDK versions:** web `@supabase/supabase-js` **2.118.0** / `auth-js` **2.118.0**; mobile `supabase_flutter` **2.18.3** → `supabase` **2.16.5** → `gotrue` **2.27.3**; `flutter_secure_storage` **11.2.0**; `google_sign_in` **^7.2.0**.
   - **gotrue 2.27.3 internals:** `getSession()` refreshes an expired session and shares a single refresh; `_callRefreshToken` de-duplicates via `_pendingRefreshes[refreshToken]`; `_doRefresh` discards stale results via `_sessionVersion`, tolerates `refresh_token_already_used` when the current session is still valid, removes the session + emits `signedOut(sessionExpired)` only for non-retryable errors, emits `notifyException` for `AuthRetryableFetchException`; `_signOut` calls `_removeSession()` and clears the code-verifier **BEFORE** the network call.
   - **auth-js 2.118.0:** creates a `BroadcastChannel(storageKey)` when `isBrowser() && persistSession && storageKey`; `_notifyAllSubscribers(event, session, broadcast=true)` posts every event cross-tab; incoming cross-tab events use `broadcast=false` and fire immediately.
   - **Supabase access-token claims** (confirmed via Supabase docs): `iss, exp, sub, role, email, phone` (+ `aal`, `amr`, `session_id`, `app_metadata`, `user_metadata`). **No `email_verified` claim.**
   - **Environment quirks:** proxy bypass list `{'no': 'localhost,.localhost,127.0.0.1,127.0.0.0/8,[::1],::1'}` makes installed httpx raise `InvalidURL: Invalid port: ':1]'` → workaround `NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1 no_proxy=localhost,127.0.0.1` in the process env only. Git Bash `/tmp` ≠ Windows python `/tmp` (MSYS path mangling) → use explicit `C:/Users/Lenovo/AppData/Local/Temp/...` paths when handing files between curl and python. Windows venv `python.exe` is a launcher shim (parent/child PID pairs). Git Bash background wrappers get reaped with exit 127 while uvicorn survives.
   - 007 skill methodology: 6 phases (Surface Mapping → STRIDE+PASTA → Technical Checklist → Red Team → Blue Team → Verdict), weighted scoring, verdicts 90-100 Approved / 70-89 Approved with reservations / 50-69 Partial block / 0-49 Total block. Its scripts hardcode the wrong user path `C:\Users\renat\skills\007\scripts\`; real base dir is `C:\Users\Lenovo\.agents\skills\007`.

       ```js
       getItem: key => sessionStorage.getItem(key) ?? localStorage.getItem(key),
       ```

       ```js
       const SESSION_COOKIE = 'sitewise_session';
       const TOKEN_COOKIE = 'sitewise_token';
       function writeCookie(name, value) {
         const secure = location.protocol === 'https:' ? '; Secure' : '';
         document.cookie = `${name}=${value}; Path=/; SameSite=Lax; Max-Age=28800${secure}`;
       }
       function clearCookie(name) { document.cookie = `${name}=; Path=/; SameSite=Lax; Max-Age=0`; }
       async function syncSessionCookies() {
         const { data } = await client.auth.getSession();
         if (!data.session) return clearSessionCookies();
         writeCookie(TOKEN_COOKIE, data.session.access_token);
         if (state.profile) writeCookie(SESSION_COOKIE, encodeSession(state.profile));
       }
       ```
       No `HttpOnly` (impossible from JS), fixed 8 h `Max-Age` independent of JWT `exp`, `Path=/` so it rides every same-origin request.

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **`ImportError: cannot import name 'SessionLocal' from 'sitewise.db.session'`** — read `db/session.py`; it exports `engine` and `get_db`. Fixed by using `from sqlmodel import Session; from sitewise.db.session import engine; with Session(engine) as s:`.
   - **`FileNotFoundError: '/tmp/sw_openapi.json'`** — Git Bash curl wrote to its own `/tmp` mount while Windows python resolved `/tmp` to `C:\tmp`. Fixed by writing to an explicit `C:/Users/Lenovo/AppData/Local/Temp/sw_auth_audit/openapi.json` and opening the Windows path in python.
   - **`grep: pubspec.lock: No such file or directory`** and **`.dart_tool/package_config.json` missing** — resolved versions instead from the pub cache: `supabase_flutter-2.18.3/pubspec.yaml` → `supabase: 2.16.5` → `supabase-2.16.5/pubspec.yaml` → `gotrue: 2.27.3`.
   - **`ls: cannot access 'config/'` in fyp_mobile_app** — the directory does not exist, so `run-sitewise.ps1:45`'s `--dart-define-from-file=fyp_mobile_app/config/dev.env` reference is stale. Logged as a P2 finding, not "fixed".
   - **curl exit 7 (connection refused) on all :8090 probes** — the server had stopped. Verified no listeners on 8090/8000/5173/8080 and no `python.exe` processes (tree free, satisfying "Do not terminate existing servers or rebuild a shared deployment directory without checking whether another session is using it"), then restarted `run-sitewise.ps1` in the background. Health returned 200 and all probes succeeded.
   - **`test_callback_flows.js` 2 failures** — investigated rather than assuming a product bug. Root cause: the tests encode the pre-`joel10` invitation contract; `auth_client.js:1151` intentionally replaced local sign-out + notice with `finishStaffOnboarding()`. Git history confirms the test was last touched in `ddadce9 joel9` while the implementation changed in `c1e69d8 joel10` / `8f7b26e joel15`. Reported as a **stale-test P2**, not an auth bypass.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Local PostgreSQL 18.4** on localhost:5432, database `Final_Location_Analysis` (**46 GB**), extensions PostGIS 3.6.2, postgis_raster 3.6.2, pgrouting 4.0.1, fuzzystrmatch 1.2, hstore 1.8, postgis_topology 3.6.2, plpgsql 1.0. Other databases on the server: `postgis_36_sample` (29 MB), `postgres` (7857 kB), template0/template1. **No `sitesense` database exists.**
   - **`DATABASE_URL` and `ANALYTICS_DATABASE_URL` are identical** — both `postgresql+psycopg2://…@localhost:5432/Final_Location_Analysis`. There is no separate analytics server.
   - **12 user schemas, 373 tables:** public (153 tables + 5 views, 25 GB), fyp_features (80, 6.2 GB), popfeat (19 + 1 view, 7.9 GB), fnb_a (60, 1.8 GB), rcv_2026_11_09 (23, 45 MB), fyp (17, 5.2 MB), demand_gen (7, 279 MB), mrf_2026_11_06 (7, 7.8 MB), sitewise_analytics (2, 44 MB), sitewise_app (2, 3.4 MB), tmp (1, 0.4 MB), topology (PostGIS-managed).
   - **`nighttime_2026_11_07` DOES NOT EXIST.** **`public.property` DOES NOT EXIST.**
   - **Locally: 1 non-internal trigger (PostGIS `topology.layer :: layer_integrity_checks`), 0 RLS policies, 0 application triggers.** `pg_stat_database.stats_reset` is NULL (counters cumulative, never reset).
   - **Three property "universes"** by row count: **894** (public.pf_*, pr_*, pz_*, psense_nearest_station/outlets, sw_positive_fnb_population_features, fyp_features.positives/pos_building), **955** (sitewise_app.properties, fyp.property_subject_links, fyp.property_listing_sources, public.pzu_*, u955_*, uf_*, up_*, ur_*, usense_props, rcv_*.rx_*_955), **1849** (demand_gen.subjects/features, sitewise_analytics.subject_features, mrf_2026_11_06.building_density, rcv_*.rx_*_1849). Also fyp.rental_properties = 1187; fnb_a outlets = 19419 / candidates = 19538.
   - **Only tables with substantial runtime scans:** fyp_features.property_population_spatial (idx 248036), sitewise_analytics.subject_features (155918), fyp.property_subject_links (42448), fyp.rental_properties (42336), public.users (seq 2888, ins 137), public.recommendation_result (seq 258, ins 126), public.entrepreneur_preferences (seq 246, ins 4), public.favorite_property (seq 158, ins 2), public.assessment (seq 149), sitewise_app.properties (seq 75, idx 144), demand_gen.features (idx 78), public.district_population (seq 12), public.agent_listing_assignment (seq 11, idx 5, ins 5, 0 live rows), public.virtual_tour (seq 7), fnb_a.fnb_outlet (idx 2), fnb_a.source_snapshot (seq 1). Empty-but-scanned (seq 137/274): ai_explanation, listing_change_audit, listing_change_request, property_verification, user_tokens, verification_audit, verification_document.
   - **Supabase project `lzdpjwmcjbhtkzkqudyt`**; tables the backend actually uses: `users`, `agent`, `company`, `listing`, `listing_verification`, `staff_invitations`, `role_change_audit`. **Properties/listings live in Supabase `public.listing`**, while local has `sitewise_app.properties` (955) and `fyp.rental_properties` (1187) → three-way property store. **Two assignment mechanisms:** Supabase `listing.agent_id` (integer) vs local `public.agent_listing_assignment`.
   - **Supabase triggers declared in repo migrations:** `on_auth_user_created` (AFTER INSERT ON auth.users) → `public.handle_new_auth_user()`; `users_identity_protection` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.users) → `public.enforce_users_identity_protection()`; `users_touch_updated_at` → `public.touch_updated_at()`; plus `public.sitewise_audit_user_role_status()`. **Neither `on_auth_user_profile_sync` nor `private.handle_auth_user_profile()` appears anywhere in the repo** — unverified against the live DB.
   - **The Supabase provisioning trigger writes ONLY to Supabase `public.users`** — it cannot reach the separate local PostgreSQL server. The only local sync path is the backend's lazy `get_or_create_from_supabase` at login.
   - **Live `role_change_audit` columns:** `user_id_affected`, `affected_email_snapshot`, `changed_by_user_id`, `entity_type`, `entity_id` (uuid), `action`, `action_status`, `old_role`, `new_role`, `old_account_status`, `new_account_status`, non-null `details` jsonb, `changed_at`. Historical names cause PostgREST **PGRST204**.
   - **Unapplied migration candidate:** `20261107133000_m2_upstream_foundation.sql` would create 9 `m2_*` tables (m2_instrument_versions, m2_item_versions, m2_assessments, m2_answer_revisions, m2_bdj_evaluations, m2_bdj_findings, m2_review_events, m2_confirmation_snapshots, m2_confirmed_uncertainties) — none exist locally.
   - **Deployment:** ONE FastAPI server on 127.0.0.1:8090 (`run-sitewise.cmd`/`.ps1` → uvicorn `sitewise.main:app`), serving the compiled client-mode Jaspr bundle from `fyp_jaspr_web/build/jaspr` plus `/api/v1/*`, `/health`, `/docs`, `/openapi.json`. Does NOT rebuild Jaspr/Flutter. The SSR launcher `fyp_jaspr_web/serve.cmd` (with `createApiProxy` + `createStaffGuard`) is separate and dormant.
   - **Backend auth chain:** `bearer_token` → `get_jwt_verifier()` → `SupabaseJWTVerifier` (local JWKS, ES256, leeway 30, required claims sub/exp/iat/aud/iss) or `SupabaseUserinfoVerifier` fallback → `get_current_user` → `UserService.get_or_sync_current` → `get_active_user` → `get_staff_user` (`rbac.is_staff` + `aal == "aal2"`, else 403 `{"code":"staff_mfa_required"}`). Role/status always from the local DB row.
   - **Auth-audit root causes (still unfixed, awaiting approval):** P0 `user_repository.py:97` hardcodes `role=entrepreneur, account_status=active` for any unseen Supabase UUID; P1 `user_service.py:73` never reconciles role/status on login; P1 `security.py:176` reads a nonexistent `email_verified` JWT claim (Supabase issues no such claim; `conftest.py:95` injects it, masking the bug); P1 `mobile_api_client.dart::_bearer()` uses `currentSession?.accessToken` with no refresh-and-retry.
   - **Python env:** sqlalchemy 2.1.6, sqlmodel 0.0.48, **psycopg2 2.9.13** (psycopg3 ABSENT), supabase 2.34.0, httpx 0.28.1, alembic 1.20.1. **`psql` and `pg_dump` are ABSENT.**
   - **Sandbox constraint discovered:** Bash-spawned processes can only write inside the workspace. `tempfile.gettempdir()` returns `C:\Users\Lenovo\AndroidStudioProjects\FYP\backend`. The Write tool CAN write to Temp, but python cannot. Git-ignored scratch dir chosen: **`backend/.venv/.sw_db_audit`** (matched by `backend/.gitignore:4:.venv/`).
   - **psycopg2 gotcha:** passing an empty tuple as query vars makes psycopg2 interpolate literal `%` in `LIKE 'pg_temp_%'`; pass `None` instead.
   - **Supabase MCP connector:** installed (official, v1.0.0, serverId `2cf015f2-f538-4320-8683-82be8d24e2e0`, streamableHttp, managed-gateway OAuth), authorized and `status: ready`, exposes **36 tools** including `execute_sql`, `list_tables`, `list_migrations`, `list_extensions`, `get_advisors`, `query_logs` — but **NOT loaded into this session** (full `mcp_list` = 144 tools, none supabase; `mcp_call mcp__supabase__list_projects` → "No connected MCP server offers a tool named…"). A session reload is required.
   - **PostgREST root OpenAPI trick:** `GET {SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/` with apikey+Bearer headers returns a spec whose `definitions` enumerate exposed tables, columns, types, and Primary/Foreign Key hints — a way to inventory Supabase `public` without pg_catalog.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   **NO APPLICATION FILE HAS BEEN MODIFIED.** Only scratch scripts were created (outside the repo in Temp, and copies inside the git-ignored `backend/.venv/.sw_db_audit/`).

   - **`C:\Users\Lenovo\AppData\Local\Temp\sw_db_audit\recon.py`** (created, then copied to `backend/.venv/.sw_db_audit/recon.py`) — read-only reconnaissance. Key pattern:
     ```python
     conn = psycopg2.connect(dsn(), connect_timeout=10)
     conn.set_session(readonly=True, autocommit=False)
     cur.execute("SET statement_timeout = '60s'")
     cur.execute("SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY")
     def q(sql, args=None):
         # args must stay None when absent: an empty tuple makes psycopg2
         # interpolate literal '%' in LIKE patterns as placeholders.
         cur.execute(sql, args)
         return [dict(r) for r in cur.fetchall()]
     ```
     Queries pg_database, pg_extension, pg_namespace, pg_class object counts, tables, pg_proc counts, pg_trigger (non-internal, with `pg_get_triggerdef`), pg_policies, pg_stat_database.stats_reset. Writes `local_recon.json`.

   - **`backend/.venv/.sw_db_audit/inventory.py`** (+ Temp original) — full inventory. `EXACT_LIMIT_BYTES = 64*1024*1024`; exact `COUNT(*)` for tables ≤64 MB or `est_rows < 0`, else reltuples marked `~`; on error rolls back and re-sets `statement_timeout`/`READ ONLY`. Collects pg_stat_user_tables (seq_scan, idx_scan, n_tup_ins/upd/del, n_live_tup, n_dead_tup, last_autovacuum/autoanalyze/analyze) and views via `pg_get_viewdef(c.oid, true)`. Outputs `local_inventory.json` and **`inventory.txt` (403 lines)**. Section start lines: demand_gen 2, fnb_a 11, fyp 73, fyp_features 92, mrf_2026_11_06 174, popfeat 183, public 204, rcv_2026_11_09 359, sitewise_analytics 384, sitewise_app 388, tmp 392, VIEWS 395.

   - **`backend/supabase/migrations/repair_auth_user_provisioning_live.sql`** (read in full, 80 lines) — explains GoTrue `invite_user_by_email` failing with "Database error saving new user"; idempotent repair:
     ```sql
     create or replace function public.handle_new_auth_user()
     returns trigger language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public
     as $fn$
     begin
       insert into public.users (
         user_id, email, full_name, role, account_status, email_verified,
         created_at, updated_at)
       values (
         new.id, coalesce(new.email, ''),
         coalesce(new.raw_user_meta_data ->> 'full_name',
                  new.raw_user_meta_data ->> 'name'),
         'entrepreneur',
         case when new.email_confirmed_at is not null then 'active' else 'pending' end,
         new.email_confirmed_at is not null, now(), now())
       on conflict (user_id) do nothing;
       return new;
     end $fn$;
     drop trigger if exists on_auth_user_created on auth.users;
     create trigger on_auth_user_created after insert on auth.users
       for each row execute function public.handle_new_auth_user();
     ```
     Includes read-only PRE-CHECK and POST-CHECK queries. **Note the Supabase trigger correctly sets `pending` when email is unconfirmed — strictly better than the local backend's hardcoded `active`.**

   - **`backend/supabase/migrations/20261105090000_role_model_staff_mfa.up.sql`** (422 lines; read 185–294) — §4 `enforce_users_identity_protection()` (service_role/supabase_admin/postgres bypass; INSERT always raises "Profile provisioning is restricted to the Sitewise backend."; changes to user_id/role/account_status/email_verified raise; `auth.uid() <> OLD.user_id` raises "You may only update your own profile."), BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger `users_identity_protection`. §5 `handle_new_auth_user()` SECURITY DEFINER, same provisioning without explicit timestamps, ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, comment "the backend's lazy provisioning stays a safe fallback". §6 `public.staff_invitations` DDL with `invited_user_id uuid REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL`, `invited_by_user_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.users(user_id)`, status CHECK ('pending','sent','accepted','expired','revoked'), indexes + `UNIQUE staff_invitations_one_open_per_email … WHERE invitation_status IN ('pending','sent')`, RLS enabled with NO policies.

   - **`backend/supabase/migrations/20261107120000_users_foundation.sql`** (full 25 lines) — `CREATE TABLE public.users` with the 11 columns (user_id UUID PK, full_name, email VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL, phone_number, role VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, profile_picture_url, account_status VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL, email_verified BOOLEAN NOT NULL, last_login, created_at NOT NULL, updated_at NOT NULL). Critical assignment code:
     ```python
     def assign_listing(self, listing_id: int, agent_id: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
         result = (self.client.table("listing")
                   .update({"agent_id": agent_id})
                   .eq("listing_id", listing_id).execute())
         if not result.data: raise NotFoundError("Listing not found.")
     ```

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **`IndexError: tuple index out of range` in recon.py** — `cur.execute(sql, args or ())` passed an empty tuple, so psycopg2 treated `%` in `LIKE 'pg_temp_%'` as a placeholder. Fixed by passing `args` (None) directly, with a comment explaining the gotcha.
   - **`PermissionError: [Errno 13]` writing to Temp** — probed and found Bash-spawned python cannot write to `Temp`, `Temp/sw_db_audit`, `Temp/sw_auth_audit`, `C:\Users\Lenovo\.qoder` or `C:\Users\Lenovo`, but CAN write to the workspace root and `backend`. `tempfile.gettempdir()` returns the backend dir, confirming a workspace-only write sandbox. Fixed by creating the git-ignored scratch dir **`backend/.venv/.sw_db_audit`** (verified with `git check-ignore -v` → `backend/.gitignore:4:.venv/`) and copying scripts there before running. Rejected `backend/.pytest_cache` (ignored but could be wiped by the concurrent session's pytest run).
   - **Supabase MCP tools not callable** — install succeeded (`runtimeReady: true`), OAuth completed (`status: ready`), `list_tools` shows 36 tools, but `mcp_list` returns 0 and `mcp_call mcp__supabase__list_projects` errors "No connected MCP server offers a tool named…". A `retry` action was accepted but did not help; the full 144-tool catalog confirms the server is absent from this session. **Not fixed** — reported honestly to the user that a session reload is required, and fell back to the backend's own service-role adapter plus the PostgREST root OpenAPI for table discovery. Supabase pg_catalog metadata, live triggers and RLS policies remain **NOT VERIFIED**.
   - **Carried-forward environment quirks (already diagnosed, still relevant):** proxy bypass list containing `[::1]` makes httpx raise `InvalidURL: Invalid port: ':1]'` → set `NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1 no_proxy=localhost,127.0.0.1`; Git Bash `/tmp` ≠ Windows python `/tmp` (MSYS path mangling) → use explicit Windows paths; Git Bash background wrappers get reaped with exit 127 while uvicorn survives (re-confirmed this segment: `/health` 200 after b3toknbed's exit-127).
   - **No corrective user feedback in this segment.** The only user-authored inputs were the attachment message and the three AskUserQuestion selections.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Confirmed the server survived** the exit-127 notification by probing `/health` (200) and reading the task output tail, rather than assuming either outcome.

   - **psycopg2 gotcha:** pass `None` (not an empty tuple) as query vars, else literal `%` in `LIKE 'pg_temp_%'` is treated as a placeholder.

A session reload is required. **No Supabase Postgres DSN is configured anywhere** → pg_catalog/trigger/RLS inspection is blocked.
   - **PostgREST root OpenAPI trick:** `GET {SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/` with apikey+Bearer returns `definitions` (columns/types/required/PK-FK hints) and `paths` (`/rpc/*`) — a pg_catalog-free inventory route. Exact counts via `Prefer: count=exact` + `Range: 0-0` → `content-range`.

   - **`backend/src/sitewise/api/v1/staff.py:930-1056`** (READ) — proves the hybrid assignment design and the broken verification path:
     ```python
     def _require_listing_access(session, service, user, listing_id):
         repo = AgentAssignmentRepository(session)
         assignment = repo.get(listing_id)
         if rbac.can_administer_users(user.role):
             if assignment is not None: return assignment, None
             exists = session.execute(text("SELECT 1 FROM fyp.rental_properties WHERE id = :lid"), {"lid": int(listing_id)}).scalar()
             if not exists: raise HTTPException(404, "Listing not found.")
             return {"listing_id": int(listing_id), "agent_id": None, "property_exists": True}, None
         if assignment is None: raise HTTPException(404, "Listing not found.")
         profile = _agent_profile_or_empty(service, user.user_id)
         if profile is None or assignment.get("agent_id") != profile.get("agent_id"):
             raise HTTPException(403, "This listing is not assigned to you.")
         return assignment, profile
     ```
     `POST /listings/{id}/verifications` (:988) → `service.insert_listing_verification(...)` → Supabase `listing_verification` → **PGRST205**. `GET` (:1007) → `list_listing_verifications` → same. `POST /listings/{id}/assign` (:1027) → `AgentAssignmentRepository(session).assign(listing_id, int(profile["agent_id"]), assigned_by=admin.user_id)` → LOCAL. `list_assigned_listings`/`get_listing`/`assign_listing` service methods are called **only** from `staff.py.before-staff-fetch-fix.bak` → dead code.

   - **`backend/src/sitewise/repositories/recommendation_candidate_repository.py:1-120`** (READ) — the canonical recommendation source query and its documented integrity decisions:
     ```sql
     FROM sitewise_app.properties p
     LEFT JOIN LATERAL (
         SELECT l.property_id AS listing_id,
                (rp.is_published AND rp.verification_status = 'approved') AS detail_available
         FROM fyp.property_subject_links l
         JOIN fyp.rental_properties rp ON rp.id = l.property_id
         WHERE l.sitewise_property_id = p.id AND l.match_verified IS TRUE LIMIT 1
     ) link ON TRUE
     LEFT JOIN LATERAL (SELECT f.* FROM sitewise_analytics.subject_features f
         WHERE f.canonical_property_id = p.id AND btrim(f.sample_class) = 'U'
         ORDER BY f.imported_at DESC NULLS LAST LIMIT 1) sf ON TRUE
     LEFT JOIN LATERAL (SELECT q.* FROM fyp_features.property_population_spatial q
         WHERE q.source_record_id = p.id
         ORDER BY (q.source_record_type <> 'SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY'), q.source_record_type LIMIT 1) pop ON TRUE
     WHERE p.rental_rm IS NOT NULL AND p.rental_rm > 0 AND p.size_sqft IS NOT NULL AND p.size_sqft > 0
       AND coalesce(p.source_data->>'property_availability_status','UNKNOWN_NOT_VERIFIED') NOT IN :unavailable
     ```
     Docstring: "``fyp_features.property_population_spatial`` holds TWO rows per catalogue id … ``SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY`` is preferred because it is byte-identical to the PU model's training record … the previous alphabetical ``ORDER BY source_record_type`` picked the ``PROPERTY_UNLABELED`` twin, which differs in the 4th significant digit." and "The 955 imported ``fyp.rental_properties`` rows are ``verification_status='pending'`` and are deliberately NOT mixed in". Also: "Live inference for arbitrary catalogue ids is impossible from the current database because 8 of the 47 saved features … are not stored as columns".

   - **`backend/src/sitewise/ml/property_features.py:110-194`** (READ) — `_resolve(property_id, source)`:
     ```python
     if source == "rental":
         rental_id = int(property_id)   # ValueError -> FeatureResolutionError
         match = _one(_rows(self.session, """SELECT l.subject_id, l.sitewise_property_id
             FROM fyp.property_subject_links l
             WHERE l.property_id = :id AND l.match_verified IS TRUE LIMIT 2""", {"id": rental_id}), ...)
         if match is None: raise FeatureResolutionError("No verified analytical mapping for rental property")
     # catalog: never cast a catalogue ID into a rental BIGINT
     ```
     `get()` then requires `sf["sample_class"] == "U"` ("Only unlabeled commercial property subjects may be scored"), reads `demand_gen.features`, and prefers the SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY population row. **Because the 227 published listings have 0 links, the rental ML path fails closed for every published listing.**

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **Bash inline-python quoting failure:** `python -c "... re.sub(r'\$2b\$12\$[A-Za-z0-9./]+', ...)"` → `/usr/bin/bash: line 16: A-Za-z0-9./: syntax error: invalid arithmetic operator (error token is "./")`. **Fixed** by writing `redact.py` as a file with the Write tool and executing it, rather than fighting shell escaping.
   - **Self-caught credential exposure:** `fyp.users` bcrypt `password_hash` values were printed by compare_identity.py. **Fixed** by scrubbing `live_identity.json` (4 hashes → `[REDACTED: bcrypt hash present]`) and `identity_report.txt` (regex → `[REDACTED]`), verified 0 remaining. The hashes must never appear in the chat report.
   - **`fnb_a.fnb_outlet_candidates` does not exist** (`relation … does not exist`). **Fixed** by listing all fnb_a tables: the 19538-row table is **`fnb_a.candidates`** (also `candidate_group`, `outlet_members`, `outlet_names` at 19538).
   - **`fyp_features.property_population_spatial.subject_id` / `.property_id` do not exist.** **Fixed** by reading its 56 columns: the key is **`(source_record_type, source_record_id)`**, e.g. `SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY / MD-04680`.
   - **Supabase MCP still not callable** (`mcp_list keyword=supabase` → `{"tools":[],"total":0}`). **Not fixed** — no Supabase Postgres DSN is configured either, so pg_catalog, live trigger definitions and RLS policies remain **NOT VERIFIED — external prerequisite (session reload or a read-only Supabase DB connection string)**. Worked around via the PostgREST root OpenAPI + exact-count headers + behavioural audit-row evidence.
   - **Carried-forward environment quirks (already diagnosed):** proxy bypass list containing `[::1]` makes httpx raise `InvalidURL: Invalid port: ':1]'` → always set `NO_PROXY=localhost,127.0.0.1 no_proxy=localhost,127.0.0.1`; Git Bash `/tmp` ≠ Windows python `/tmp`; Bash background wrappers get reaped with exit 127 while uvicorn survives; python cannot write outside the workspace.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Proved the documented three-database architecture was never realized:** README, `supabase_admin.py` and `06_DATABASE_INTEGRATION.md` all reference a local `sitesense` DB that does not exist; `pg_stat_activity` shows all 11 backends on `Final_Location_Analysis` as superuser `postgres`.
   - **Discovered the Supabase property side is an empty shell:** `property`/`property_photo`/`pano_jobs`/`user_tokens` all 0 rows; `listing`/`listing_verification` PGRST205. Therefore the staff verification-findings endpoints are broken (502) while assignment correctly uses the local table.
   - **Established the disjoint-universe defect with exact counts:** 227 published listings (0 links, 0 photos, 212 geospatial, 207 fnb_a) vs 955 pending research imports (955 links, 5979 photos, 270 geospatial); `detail_available = 0/955`; assessments/favourites/recommendations all reference TEXT catalogue ids with no FK.
   - **Quantified the population-twin conflict** rather than accepting the code comment: 955/955 differ, max |Δ1km| 25127.875, 528 pairs >1000 — while confirming the deterministic SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY preference is correct for ML reproducibility (MD-00510 matches the training record 1071.6 / 6430.9 / 108745.1).
   - **Resolved demand_gen.features vs subject_features:** complementary, not duplicated (demand_gen has no competitor/transit/parking columns); 1849/1849 subject overlap; single source file, single snapshot date, no duplicates.
   - **Confirmed the Supabase audit trigger is live behaviourally** (NULL-actor `system_or_service_backend` rows timestamped to the exact `created_at`/`email_confirmed_at` of each identity) without pg_catalog access.
   - **Verified the spec's named trigger/function are absent from all repo migrations** but present in a prior-session live note → flagged as historical, NOT VERIFIED today.
   - **Confirmed 0 orphans across 9 referential checks** and 0 email/UUID collisions, so the integrity problems are missing FKs and key-space mismatches, not dangling rows.
   - **Confirmed the deployment is a single authenticated server** (PID 23632, /health 200, six endpoints 401) with no legacy listeners.

# REPORT A — Current architecture (verified, not as documented)

## A.1 The documented architecture is wrong in five specific ways

`backend/docs/06_DATABASE_INTEGRATION.md` (66 lines) is **stale**. Verified contradictions:

| Doc claim | Live reality | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Local application DB is **`sitesense`** | **No `sitesense` database exists.** Server has `Final_Location_Analysis` (46 GB), `postgis_36_sample` (29 MB, 0 conns), `postgres` (7857 kB, 0 conns) | `pg_database`; `pg_stat_activity` |
| Supabase is "Identity provider only … **Not connected from FastAPI** (HTTP Auth APIs only)" | FastAPI reads **and writes** Supabase tables over PostgREST with the secret key | `core/supabase_admin.py` — `.table()` on `users`, `staff_invitations`, `role_change_audit`, `company`, `agent`, `listing`, `listing_verification` |
| A separate analytics engine prevents cross-outage | `DATABASE_URL == ANALYTICS_DATABASE_URL`. Two engines, **one database, one server** | `db/session.py:6`, `db/analytics_session.py:17` |
| "`alembic/versions/` is currently empty" | 10+ migrations, **two heads** | `0010_agent_listing_assignments`, `0010_property_enrichment`, both `down_revision = 0008_recommendation_persistence` |
| public has 15 tables | public has **153** | `pg_tables` |

The same `sitesense` ghost appears in `core/supabase_admin.py:14` and `docs/01_BACKEND_GAP_ANALYSIS.md:13`.

Two doc claims **are** correct and important: *"No cross-database foreign keys … identity linkage is by UUID value, not FK"*, and *"legacy identities in `fyp.users` / `sitewise_app.profiles` are not authoritative for FastAPI auth."* The second is contradicted in practice by one FK — see A.4.

## A.4 The one cross-schema identity FK that contradicts the docs

`fyp.rental_properties.owner_id` **bigint → `fyp.users(id)`**, a real database FK. All **227** published listings have `owner_id = 8`, which is a **legacy demo row** in `fyp.users`. A second column `owner_user_id` **uuid** exists but is populated on only **3** rows (`f56fa3fa…` ×2, `f69be94e…` ×1), all of which do resolve in `public.users`.

So ownership is simultaneously modelled two ways, and the enforced one points at a 4-row legacy demo table. `fyp.users` contains ids 7–10 with roles `admin`, **`owner`**, `entrepreneur`, `entrepreneur` — and `owner` is **not** in `rbac.ALL_ROLES`.

## B.4 Deep detail on the three key-spaces (the core of the confusion)

There are **three non-interchangeable property universes**:

| Universe | Key type | Table | Count | Composition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Research catalogue** | `TEXT` | `sitewise_app.properties.id` | **955** | 732 `MD-` + 223 `EP-` |
| **Operational listings** | `BIGINT` | `fyp.rental_properties.id` | **1187** | 227 approved+published (ids 89–315) · 955 pending imports (316–1270) · 5 drafts (1961–5216) |
| **Analytics subjects** | `subject_id` | `sitewise_analytics.subject_features` | **1849** | 894 class `P` (PU training outlets) + 955 class `U` |

**The bridge is `fyp.property_subject_links` (955 rows, all `match_verified`):**

| Research ID | → rental id | Match basis | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`MD-00510`** | **316** | `WORKBOOK_EXACT_CANONICAL_ID` | U |
| **`MD-01755`** | **1139** | `WORKBOOK_EXACT_CANONICAL_ID` | U |

Both resolve correctly. **`MD-00510` is not rental id 510, and `MD-01755` is not rental id 1755.** `ml/property_features.py:110-140` explicitly refuses to cast a catalogue ID into a rental BIGINT.

**The disjoint-universe defect (exact counts):**

| Cohort | Rows | Subject links | Photos | Geospatial | `fnb_a` features |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Published + approved** (what entrepreneurs see) | **227** | **0** | **0** | 212 | 207 |
| **Pending research imports** (hidden) | **955** | **955** | **5979** | 270 | 0 |

`recommendation_candidate_repository.py`'s `detail_available` lateral evaluates to **0 of 955**. Consequence: **the rental ML path fails closed for every published listing** — `PropertyFeatureRepository._resolve(..., source="rental")` raises `FeatureResolutionError("No verified analytical mapping for rental property")` for all 227.

**Downstream tables all key to the TEXT catalogue, with no FK:**

| Table | Rows | Keys | Resolves in catalogue | Resolves in `rental_properties` | FK on `property_id` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `public.assessment` | 2 | `EP-01051`, `EP-01345` (user `f69be94e…`) | 2/2 | **0/2** | **none** |
| `public.favorite_property` | 4 | all `EP-` (2 fixture-owned) | 4/4 | **0/4** | **none** |
| `public.recommendation_result` | 14 | 7 `EP-` + 7 `MD-` (**both owners are fixtures**) | 14/14 | **0/14** | **none** |
| `public.virtual_tour` | 0 | `property_id` **integer** | — | — | **none** |
| `public.agent_listing_assignment` | 0 | `agent_id` **integer** | — | — | **none** |

# REPORT C — Usage matrix

| Database | Schema | Table | Read by | Written by | Class | Evidence |

# REPORT D — Synchronization results (reproduced live today)

## D.1 The mechanism that actually exists

| Candidate mechanism | Present? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Supabase Auth trigger → Supabase `public.users` | **Yes (behaviourally confirmed)** | 11 audit rows with `details.audit_source = 'system_or_service_backend'` and NULL actor, timestamped to each identity's `created_at`/`email_confirmed_at`. That exact JSON is built by `supabase/migrations/fix_sitewise_audit_trigger_metadata_to_details.sql:76` |
| Supabase trigger → **local** PostgreSQL | **NO** | No FDW, no `dblink`, no logical replication, no webhook, no scheduled job. A Supabase trigger cannot reach a separate server without one |
| FastAPI provisioning service | **Yes, one-way, insert-only** | `UserService.get_or_sync_current` → `user_repository.py:97` |
| Login-time reconciliation | **NO** | `user_service.py:73` never updates role/status on an existing row |
| Backend dual writes | **Partial** | Staff flows write Supabase `agent`/`company`/`staff_invitations` **and** local `users`; assignment writes local only |
| Scheduled ETL / import | **Yes, research only** | `scripts/run_data_refresh.py`, external population ETL. Never touches identities |
| PostgreSQL replication / FDW | **NO** | none configured |
| Manual SQL imports | **Yes** | `scripts/apply_*.py`, `bootstrap_sitewise_admin.py`, `bootstrap_sitewise_agent.py` |

**The spec's named objects:** `on_auth_user_profile_sync` and `private.handle_auth_user_profile()` appear in **no repo migration**. They are recorded in `.workbuddy-ai/memory/2026-11-10.md:185-224` as the *live* chain, together with `trg_sitewise_audit_user_role_status` and `trg_sitewise_protect_user_security_fields`. That note is **historical, not re-verified today** — reading the live definitions is blocked (§0). What I *can* confirm today is the **behaviour** of the audit trigger (above) and the **absence** of any Supabase→local path.

**Failure handling:** there is no retry, no idempotency key, no conflict resolution and no dead-letter for identity sync, because the only sync is a single insert inside the login request. If Supabase is reachable but local is not, login fails; if local succeeds and a later Supabase staff write fails, the two diverge silently. Account deletion, role revocation and status changes **never propagate**.

## D.2 Identity comparison — 11 Supabase ↔ 110 local

**`auth.users` ↔ Supabase `public.users`: perfect 1:1, 11 = 11, zero orphans in either direction.**

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| 1. Supabase users missing locally | **4** |
| 2. Local profiles without a Supabase identity | **103** (all fixtures) |
| 3. Role mismatches | **2** |
| 4. Status mismatches | **0** |
| 5. Email-verification mismatches | **6** |
| 6. UUID / email conflicts | **0** (`ix_users_email` is UNIQUE) |
| 7. Duplicate user records | **0** |
| 8. Incomplete Agent provisioning | **0** structural; **2** with `created_by_user_id` NULL |
| 9. Incorrect staff-invitation activation | **1** |
| 10. Test identities mixed into production-style queries | **YES — 103 of 110 local rows (93.6%)** |

**Missing locally (4):**

| UUID (prefix) | Email | Supabase state |
|---|---|---|
| `3da08202…` | shizhe832@gmail.com | confirmed |
| `42220ce3…` | jedtanhoujie@gmail.com | confirmed |
| `32ef694a…` | tabhello1612@gmail.com | confirmed; **last_sign_in 2026-11-12 09:03:43 — today** |
| `02183dc8…` | joelcode@gmail.com | **pending, never confirmed** |

The third one signed in **today** and still has no local row — proof the sync gap is live, not historical.

**Role mismatches (2) — these two people are locked out of the staff portal:**

| UUID | Supabase role | Local role | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `3911bb2e-c829-4578-831a-5b01fd1ddefa` | **admin** | `entrepreneur` | `get_staff_user` → 403 |
| `9cc08037-4930-491d-b22a-63e03735f99a` | **admin** | `entrepreneur` | `get_staff_user` → 403 |

**Root cause (two defects, both still unfixed):**
- `repositories/user_repository.py:97` **hardcodes** `role="entrepreneur"`, `account_status="active"` on insert, ignoring the authoritative Supabase role.
- `services/user_service.py:73` never reconciles role/status for an existing row on login.

**Email-verification mismatches (6):** local `email_verified = false` while Supabase `email_confirmed_at` is set. Only `7947affa…` agrees. Contributing cause: `core/security.py:176` reads a **non-existent `email_verified` JWT claim**; `tests/conftest.py:95` injects that claim, which masks the bug in tests.

**`last_login` is never updated:** it equals `created_at` for all 4 real entrepreneurs and is **NULL** for the admin and both agents.

**MFA:** **all 11 Supabase users have `factors = []`.** `get_staff_user` requires `aal == "aal2"`. **No account can currently pass the staff gate**, regardless of role. This is an enrollment gap, not a code defect — and per your standing constraint, mobile Entrepreneur auth must not gain an MFA flow.

**Local fixtures (103):** 102 `@jwks-test.example` + 1 `@sitewise-test.invalid` (`4ce62dfc-e277-48ef-b1ad-5260b6abf1d6`, role `agent`, mfa-repro). Includes **4 fixture admins** (`c54db994…`, `fb4afb01…`, `1a2411cd…`, `053c4400…`) and 1 fixture agent. **These are not removed** — provenance and dependencies must be understood first, per your instruction.

## D.3 Agents, companies, invitations

| `agent_id` (int) | Auth UUID | Name | Company | Supabase role/status | Local role/status | `created_by_user_id` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **2** | **`8de9ed3b-1431-4603-b680-731e40f53628`** | Joel Personal | 1 | agent / active | agent / active | **NULL** |
| **3** | **`dcbc87df-0911-49ed-806c-02305441439f`** | Joel | 1 | agent / active | agent / active | **NULL** |

Both profiles are complete and consistent. **`agent_id` 2/3 is an integer PK; the UUID is the identity. They are not interchangeable** — `staff.py:1027` correctly passes `int(profile["agent_id"])` to the local assignment table, while `staff.py:930` compares against the Supabase profile's `agent_id`. **Listing assignments: 0 rows** for both agents (`public.agent_listing_assignment` is empty; `ins=5` shows earlier test writes were removed).

Companies: **1 = "Sitewise"** (internal), **2 = "Hello"** (`company_type` NULL). Note `.workbuddy-ai/memory/2026-11-10.md` records a live E2E test that provisioned an agent into company 2 and then cleaned up — consistent with company 2 existing but having no agent today.

**Invitations:**

| id | Status | Evidence of actual activation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **2** | **`sent`** | email confirmed **2026-11-10 12:43:12**; sign-in **2026-11-11 20:07**; audit `staff.activated` **2026-11-11 06:25** | **STALE — incorrect activation state** |
| 3 | `accepted` | consistent | correct |

Both have `sent_at = NULL` and `expires_at = NULL` → **invitations never expire**.

**Audit trail:** 11 rows. The 4 NULL-actor `system_or_service_backend` rows prove the trigger fires. **There is no audit row for either admin promotion** — a repudiation gap: the two most privileged role changes in the system are untraceable.

## D.4 Per-field authority

| Field | Authoritative source | Local copy | Drift |
|---|---|---|---|
| UUID, email, password, email confirmation, MFA factors | **Supabase `auth.users`** | `public.users` (partial) | 6 verification mismatches |
| Display name | Supabase `public.users` | `public.users` | none observed |
| **Role** | **Supabase `public.users`** (set by authorized provisioning) | `public.users.role` — **used for every authorization decision** | **2 mismatches** |
| **Account status** | Supabase `public.users` | `public.users.account_status` | 0 mismatches today, but no propagation path |
| Agent profile, company, invitation lifecycle | **Supabase** | not mirrored | by design |
| Listing assignment | **Local `public.agent_listing_assignment`** | — | Supabase has no such table |
| `last_login` | should be local | local | **never written** |

**Authorized provisioning updates vs ordinary login sync:** role/status changes belong to the staff provisioning path (`staff.py`, `bootstrap_sitewise_*.py`) and must be audited. Login must **read-through and reconcile**, never promote. Nothing in the current code distinguishes the two — `user_repository.py:97` simply hardcodes.

## E.2 The exact write-one-table / read-another inconsistencies

**(a) Photos — dead duplicate repository.** `api/v1/properties.py:351,403,414,432` → `FypPropertyRepository` → **`fyp.property_photos`** (5979 rows). Meanwhile `repositories/property_photo_repository.py:6` targets **`public.property_photo`** (0 rows) and **has no importer anywhere in the backend**. Same for `repositories/property_feature_repository.py` → `public.property_feature` (0 rows), no importer.

**(b) Competitors — two mounted endpoints, two different sources, one always empty.**
- `GET /api/v1/explore/properties/{id}/competitors` (`explore.py:140`) → `FypPropertyRepository` → **live geodesic PostGIS** over `fnb_a.fnb_outlet` / `overture_places`, with the canonical predicate `geom IS NOT NULL AND in_target_region AND in_main_scope` (`fyp_property_repository.py:51`). **Works.**
- `GET /api/v1/geospatial/property/{id}/competitors` (`geospatial.py:33`) → `GeospatialRepository.get_competitors_by_property_id` → **`public.nearby_competitor`**, **0 rows**. **Always empty.** Same for `/geo-feature` → `public.property_geospatial_feature` (0) and `/foot-traffic` → `public.property_foot_traffic` (0).

Both routers are mounted (`api/v1/router.py:68,75`). A client choosing the `/geospatial` prefix gets silence where `/explore` gets real data.

**(c) Staff verification findings — writes to a table that does not exist.** `staff.py:988` `POST /listings/{id}/verifications` → `service.insert_listing_verification` → `supabase_admin.py:725` `.table("listing_verification")` → **PGRST205**. `staff.py:1007` `GET` → `:736` → same. Meanwhile `staff.py:1027` `POST /listings/{id}/assign` → `AgentAssignmentRepository.assign` → **local `public.agent_listing_assignment`** → works. The staff UI therefore has a functioning assignment action beside a permanently failing verification action.

**(d) Dead service methods.** `list_assigned_listings`, `get_listing`, `assign_listing` in the Supabase admin service are called **only** from `api/v1/staff.py.before-staff-fetch-fix.bak` — a backup file, not a mounted module.

**(e) Ownership.** `fyp.rental_properties.owner_id` (enforced FK → `fyp.users`) vs `owner_user_id` (uuid, unenforced, 3 rows). The enforced column points at a demo identity for all 227 published listings.

| **`public.agent_listing_assignment`** | 0 | `agent_id` integer, **no FK**; `listing_id` no FK | Add FKs to `fyp.rental_properties(id)` and a Supabase-UUID-keyed agent reference |
| **`public.virtual_tour`** | 0 | `property_id` **integer**, no FK; catalogue keys are TEXT | Decide the key-space, then add the FK |

## E.4 Conflicting duplicate feature values — quantified

`fyp_features.property_population_spatial` holds **6410** rows keyed by `(source_record_type, source_record_id)`:

| `source_record_type` | Rows |
|---|---|
| `PROPERTY_UNLABELED` | 4561 |
| `SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY` | **955** |
| `FNB_POSITIVE` | 894 |

**All 955 catalogue ids have a twin, and all 955 pairs disagree:**

| Metric on `pop_total_1km` | Value |
|---|---|
| mean abs difference | **3070.648 people** |
| median | 1457.945 |
| p95 | 11965.838 |
| **max** | **25127.875** |
| pairs differing by > 1000 people | **528 of 955** |

The code comment in `recommendation_candidate_repository.py` and `ml/property_features.py` claims the twin *"differs in the 4th significant digit."* **That is true only for `MD-00510`** (1071.6 / 6430.9 / 108745.1, matching the supplied `example_property_47.json` training record byte-for-byte). It is **false as a general statement**.

**The deterministic preference is nonetheless correct**: `ORDER BY (source_record_type <> 'SITEWISE_U_PROPERTY'), source_record_type` picks the training-identical row, which is what ML reproducibility requires. The defect is the **comment**, and the **absence of any documented decision** about the other 954 pairs.

**Density is not a count.** The table carries both, and they must never be conflated:
- counts: `pop_ring_0_500m`, `pop_total_1km`, `pop_total_3km`
- densities: `pop_density_km2`, `pop_density_500m_km2`

**`demand_gen.features` vs `sitewise_analytics.subject_features` are complementary, not duplicated.** Both have 1849 rows with full subject overlap, but `demand_gen.features` has **no competitor, transit or parking columns**. Both come from a single source workbook, single snapshot date **2026-11-03**, all rows COMPLETED, no duplicate keys. **No stale-import or version conflict found here.**

## E.5 Which tables feed which layer

| Layer | Input tables | Output kind | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Layer 1 — PU association score** | `sitewise_app.properties.source_data->>'research_p_association_score'` for the 955 catalogue rows; **live inference** via `sitewise_analytics.subject_features` + `demand_gen.features` + `fyp_features.property_population_spatial` + `ml_reference/shap_background_47.csv` | **Stored research prediction** for the catalogue (verified byte-for-byte against live `pu_model` inference for `MD-00510`); **live prediction** on `/ml/...` and `/ml/selected/...` | `recommendation_candidate_repository.py` docstring; `selected_pu_routes.py:44,76` |
| **Layer 2 — preference ranking** | `public.entrepreneur_preferences` (6) + Layer-1 scores + eligibility filters | **Rule-based, versioned** — explicitly *not* a trained recommender | `domain/recommendation_scoring.py`; `recommendation_generation_service.py:157` |
| **Layer 3 — SHAP explanation** | Same 47-feature bundle + `ml_reference/shap_background_47.csv`; persisted in `public.ai_explanation` | **Live SHAP** over the research PU model, plus separate rule-engine reasons. SHAP never explains the rules | `selected_pu_routes.py:92-122`; `personalised_recommendations.py:118-135`; `explanation_service.py:175` |

**Reproducibility caveat (documented in the code, confirmed):** live inference for an arbitrary catalogue id is **impossible from stored columns alone** — 8 of the 47 features (population rings, LandScan ambient, WorldPop walk-reachable) are not stored as columns and are resolved from `fyp_features.property_population_spatial` plus the training bridge. The `/ml/...` endpoints **fail closed** when a feature cannot be resolved; nothing is zero-filled. `selected_pu_routes.py:84-88` states this policy explicitly.

**Availability is never fabricated:** all 955 catalogue rows are `UNVERIFIED_RESEARCH_DATASET`; `UNKNOWN_NOT_VERIFIED` is surfaced as a caution, never as confirmed availability.

# REPORT F — Recommended architecture

## F.1 Entity classification and synchronization matrix

| Entity | Supabase table | Local table | Source of truth | Expected synchronization | **Actual status** | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Users (Entrepreneur)** | `auth.users` + `public.users` (11) | `public.users` (110, 7 real) | **Supabase** for identity; **local** for role/status enforcement | Login-time read-through reconcile, insert-if-missing | **Broken** — 4 missing, 2 role drift, 6 verification drift, 103 fixtures | **P0/P1** |
| **Admin** | `public.users.role='admin'` (2) | `public.users` (2 real + 4 fixture) | **Supabase**, via audited provisioning | Explicit authorized promotion + audit row | **Broken** — both local rows say `entrepreneur`; **no audit rows** | **P0** |
| **Agent** | `public.agent` (2) + `public.users` | `public.users` (2 real + 1 fixture) | **Supabase** for profile; **local** for assignment | Provision on invite acceptance; reconcile on login | **Consistent** for 2/3; `created_by_user_id` NULL; invitation 2 stale | **P1** |
| **Property owner** | — | `fyp.rental_properties.owner_id` → **`fyp.users`**; `owner_user_id` uuid (3 rows) | **Should be Supabase UUID** | Application-level UUID contract | **Broken** — enforced FK points at a 4-row demo table; all 227 published rows owned by demo id 8 | **P0** |
| **Rental property (operational)** | `public.property` (**0**) | **`fyp.rental_properties`** (1187) | **Local** | **None required** — deliberately local-only | **Correct by design**; Supabase shell is obsolete | **P2** retire the shell |
| **Research catalogue** | — | **`sitewise_app.properties`** (955) | **Local** | None | Correct | Retain |
| **Property analytics** | — | `sitewise_analytics.subject_features`, `demand_gen.features`, `fyp_features.property_population_spatial`, `fyp.property_geospatial_features` | **Local** | None | Correct; population twins need a documented decision | **P1** |
| **Assessment** | not reachable | **`public.assessment`** (2) | **Local** | None | Correct source; **no FK**; keys are TEXT catalogue ids while Explore serves BIGINT | **P1** |
| **Recommendation** | not reachable | **`public.recommendation_result`** (14) | **Local** | None | Correct source; **no FK**; both owners are fixtures | **P1/P2** |
| **Virtual tour** | not reachable | **`public.virtual_tour`** (0) | **Local** | None | `property_id` integer, no FK, key-space undecided | **P1** |
| **Media / photos** | `public.property_photo` (**0**) | **`fyp.property_photos`** (5979) | **Local** | None | Correct; public twin is dead | **P2** |
| **Listing assignment** | not reachable | **`public.agent_listing_assignment`** (0) | **Local** | None | Correct source; no FKs | **P1** |
| **Staff invitations** | **`public.staff_invitations`** (2) | — | **Supabase** | None | Live schema ≠ repo migration; invitation 2 stale; never expires | **P1** |
| **Role-change audit** | **`public.role_change_audit`** (11) | — | **Supabase** | None | Trigger fires; **admin promotions unaudited** | **P0** |
| **Companies** | **`public.company`** (2) | — | **Supabase** | None | Correct | Retain |
| **Tokens / sessions** | Supabase Auth | `public.user_tokens` (0) | **Supabase Auth** | None | Local table obsolete | **P2** |

**Per your instruction: not every Supabase table needs a local counterpart.** Properties, analytics, assessments, recommendations, tours and assignments are **deliberately local-only**. Users, admins, agents, companies, invitations and audit are **deliberately Supabase-authoritative**. The only genuinely *synchronized-mirrored* entity is **Users** — and that is the one that is broken.

## F.2 Target design

1. **Two databases, honestly.** Either (a) create the `sitesense` application database the docs already promise and point `DATABASE_URL` at it, keeping `Final_Location_Analysis` for analytics; or (b) delete the `sitesense` claim from `06_DATABASE_INTEGRATION.md`, `01_BACKEND_GAP_ANALYSIS.md` and `supabase_admin.py:14`, and document the single-database reality. **(b) is far cheaper and is what I recommend for an FYP.** Option (a) is a real migration with real risk.
2. **One identity authority.** Supabase owns identity, role and status. Local `public.users` is a **cache** that is reconciled on every login (read-through, never promote), with `last_login` written and a `synced_at` column.
3. **One property key-space per surface, explicitly bridged.** Explore/details = BIGINT `fyp.rental_properties`. Catalogue/assessment/recommendation = TEXT `sitewise_app.properties`. The bridge is `fyp.property_subject_links`, and **every cross-surface payload must carry both ids**.
4. **One canonical table per concept**, with the duplicates in E.1 retired after migration.
5. **Least privilege.** Replace the `postgres` superuser connection with an app role that has DML on `public`/`fyp`/`sitewise_app` and **SELECT only** on the research schemas. This alone makes the "analytics is read-only" claim true.
6. **Research schemas are a frozen archive.** `popfeat`, `fyp_features`, `rcv_*`, `mrf_*`, `public.psense_*` etc. become SELECT-only for the app role, documented as reproducibility evidence, and are never dropped.
7. **Alembic is the single schema authority.** Merge the two heads, stamp the true version, remove the three runtime-DDL sites and the startup `create_all`.

# REPORT H — Non-destructive cleanup & migration plan

## ⚠️ THIS PLAN IS NOT EXECUTED. No step below has been run.

It follows the convention already established by `docs/CLEANUP_20261110.md`: explicit owner approval → backup outside the repo → SHA-256 + CRC manifest → dependency check → change → verification → rollback.

### Phase 0 — Prerequisites (blocking)

1. **Obtain read-only Supabase `pg_catalog` access** (session reload for the MCP, or a read-only DSN). Without it, no Supabase object may be touched.
2. **Locate and interview the external ETL** at `C:/Users/Lenovo/OneDrive/Desktop/JOEL/Prompt File/Sitewise_Positive_Population_Completion` and the Downloads CSV. Produce its full read/write table list. **Until this exists, every research-schema candidate stays at "Insufficient evidence."**
3. **Rotate the leaked Supabase DB password and secret key** (P0-7).
4. **Full backup**: `pg_dump -Fc` of `Final_Location_Analysis` (46 GB — confirm free space; `C:` had ~6.6 GB free at last check, so **the dump must go to another volume**), plus a Supabase export of the 9 tables. Store outside the repo with a `backup_manifest.json` recording path, size and SHA-256 per file, CRC-checked.
5. **Snapshot the definitions** of every function, view and trigger to be touched (`pg_get_functiondef`, `pg_get_viewdef`, `pg_get_triggerdef`).

Create an app role with DML on `public`/`fyp`/`sitewise_app` and SELECT on the research schemas; switch `DATABASE_URL`/`ANALYTICS_DATABASE_URL` to it; verify every endpoint still works. **Keep the superuser path for migrations only.** Rollback = restore the previous environment values.

### Global rollback

Every phase is independently reversible. Phases 1–3 and 5–7 are code/config reverts plus constraint drops. Phase 4 uses rename-then-drop so nothing is destroyed until a separate approval. The Phase 0 backup is the last resort and must be **restore-tested into a scratch database** before any phase begins.

# Acceptance criteria — the ten questions

**1. Which Supabase tables are authoritative?**
`auth.users` (identity, credentials, email confirmation, MFA), `public.users` (role, account status, display name), `public.agent` (staff profile), `public.company`, `public.staff_invitations`, `public.role_change_audit`. **Six.** The other four exposed tables — `property`, `property_photo`, `pano_jobs`, `user_tokens` — are **empty shells with no authority**. `listing` and `listing_verification` are **not reachable** and the backend nonetheless writes to them.

**2. Which local tables are authoritative?**
`fyp.rental_properties` (operational listings), `fyp.property_photos` (media), `fyp.property_subject_links` (the key-space bridge), `fyp.property_geospatial_features` / `property_features` / `property_listing_sources` / `property_enrichment_category` / `property_feature_freshness`, `sitewise_app.properties` (research catalogue + Layer-1 scores), `sitewise_analytics.subject_features` (ML features), `demand_gen.features` / `subjects` / `pois_master`, `fyp_features.property_population_spatial`, `public.users` (the **enforcement** copy of role/status), `public.assessment`, `public.favorite_property`, `public.entrepreneur_preferences`, `public.recommendation_result`, `public.ai_explanation`, `public.property_verification` (+ documents/audit), `public.listing_change_request` (+ audit), `public.agent_listing_assignment`, `public.virtual_tour`, and the live `fnb_a` competitor pipeline.

**3. Which entities should exist in both?**
**Only Users** (including Admin and Agent identities) — Supabase-authoritative, locally mirrored for enforcement. **Everything else is deliberately single-database**: properties, analytics, assessments, recommendations, tours, media and assignments are local-only; companies, invitations and role audit are Supabase-only. Per your instruction, identical table counts are neither expected nor required.

**4. Are they correctly synchronized?**
**No.** `auth.users` ↔ Supabase `public.users` is a perfect 1:1. **Supabase → local is not synchronized at all**: there is no trigger, FDW, replication, webhook or job that crosses the boundary. The only path is an insert-only, role-hardcoding login upsert. Result: **4 missing identities (one signed in today), 2 role mismatches that lock out both real admins, 6 email-verification mismatches, `last_login` never written, and no propagation of status changes, role changes or deletions.**

**5. Which tables does FastAPI actually use?**
**~45 local tables** (28 read/write, 17 read-only) across `public`, `fyp`, `sitewise_app`, `sitewise_analytics`, `demand_gen`, `fyp_features`, `fnb_a` — enumerated with file:line in Report C. **7 Supabase tables**, of which **5 work** and **2 fail with PGRST205**. Both frontends use **Supabase Auth only** — zero direct table or RPC access.

**6. Which tables are duplicated?**
**26 pairs/groups** (Report E.1). The most consequential: `public.property_photo` (0) vs `fyp.property_photos` (5979); `public.property_geospatial_feature` (0) vs `fyp.property_geospatial_features` (484); `public.nearby_competitor` (0) vs live PostGIS; the eight empty `fyp` twins of populated `public` tables; `fyp.users` (4) vs `public.users` (110); and the four Supabase empty shells. Plus **955 conflicting population twins inside a single table**.

**7. Which tables are unused or obsolete?**
**~304 tables have no reference in this repository's 927 executable source files.** But per your instruction, that is **not** proof of disuse: the research ETL lives outside the repo, and `pg_stat` counters are corroborating only. Confirmed **obsolete**: `fyp.users`, `sitewise_app.profiles`, `public.address`, both `user_tokens`, the four Supabase shells, `fyp.recommendation_results`, the two dead-repository targets, and the three dead service methods. Confirmed **unused-but-required**: `fnb_a.duplicate_review`, `source_absence`, `outlet_source_ids` — all 0 rows, all actively written.

**8. Which schemas can be considered for future removal?**
**Only `tmp`** (1 scratch table) is a genuine removal candidate, and even that needs confirmation. **`rcv_2026_11_09` and `mrf_2026_11_06` look like dated runs but must be retained** — they are ML reproducibility evidence. **`popfeat`, `fyp_features`, `fnb_a`, `demand_gen`, `sitewise_analytics`, `sitewise_app`, `fyp`, `public` and `topology` must all be retained.** The realistic goal is **not removing schemas but separating them**: freeze the research tables behind a SELECT-only role, and consolidate the 26 duplicate pairs into their canonical tables. **`nighttime_2026_11_07` does not exist**, so it cannot be removed.

**9. Are recommendations, assessments and property records querying the correct sources?**
**Yes — all three query the correct authoritative tables**, and the recommendation pipeline fails closed rather than fabricating. **But three real defects sit on top of correct sourcing:** (a) assessments, favourites and recommendations key to **TEXT catalogue ids** while Explore serves **BIGINT rental ids**, with **no FK** anywhere; (b) the **227 published listings have zero subject links and zero photos**, so the rental ML path fails closed for every property an entrepreneur can actually see, and `detail_available` is 0 for all 955 catalogue rows; (c) `/geospatial/*` reads **empty duplicates** while `/explore/*` reads live data, and the staff verification endpoints write to a **non-existent Supabase table**.

**10. What is the safest architecture for the final SiteWise FYP?**
**Keep two databases, but stop pretending there are three.** Supabase = identity, staff provisioning and audit. Local `Final_Location_Analysis` = everything else, with a **least-privilege app role** that has DML on `public`/`fyp`/`sitewise_app` and **SELECT only** on the frozen research schemas. **One canonical table per concept.** **One identity authority** with login-time read-through reconciliation that never promotes. **Two explicit property key-spaces joined only by `fyp.property_subject_links`**, with both ids carried in every cross-surface payload and FKs enforced. **Alembic as the single schema authority** — merged heads, truthful stamp, no runtime DDL. **Research tables are an archive, not clutter**: they are what makes your PU scores and SHAP explanations reproducible, and they must survive.

The cheapest high-value sequence is **P0-2 → P1-2 → P0-1 → P1-4 → P1-5 → P0-3**: fix the hardcoded role, add login reconciliation, correct the two admins (with your explicit approval), repair or repoint the verification endpoints, repoint the empty geospatial repository, then drop superuser. That resolves the lockout, the sync gap and the two broken data paths **without touching a single research table**.
